# Architecture refactor plan

This plan brings the compiler and its tools in line with `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`. That document
states the contracts; its §7 "Known deviations" table lists where the tree breaks them. The
fourteen LARGE items and the SMALL groups in `code-quality-2026-10.md` give each deviation its
measurement, its proposed shape and its first PR. This plan owns what neither of them says:
**the order, the dependencies, how a step is accepted, and where each item stands.** It does not
restate measurements; follow the links.

## When the program is done

- Every row of ARCHITECTURE.md §7 is gone, except rows that wait on an owner ruling and say so.
- Every item L1–L14 is closed, or retired with a reason recorded here.
- The ratchets that measure these deviations have reached their floors, and each is a gate:
  - render-parse (`render-parse-budget`);
  - `sentinel-index-unguarded`;
  - `kind-ladder-incomplete` and `kind-ladder-split`;
  - the emitter's never-cleared state (`emitter-state-audit.vl --check`, ratcheted by L7).
- The repo's tooling follows the standing order: everything that can be written in VL is
  written in VL (Track T).

## Rules every step follows

1. **One PR is one step.** A refactor step proves it changed nothing:
   - a byte-identical seed (`cmp` after `refresh-compiler.sh`);
   - or, where code moves, IDENTITY (compile(candidate, master source) == master fixpoint,
     which `cb-audit` checks) plus identical `vl check` text and emitted modules over
     `tests/cases` and the distilled corpus.
2. **A step that changes behaviour** must show:
   - no `runs → not-runs` in `regress.py`, and no new silent cell;
   - for a capability, a `capability-matrix.vl` position matrix in both faces, with a template that leaves
     a type parameter open.
3. **Cost is a gate, not a note.** A slower compiler is rejected however clean the code. Each
   step is checked against:
   - L2 self-compile fuel (`self-compile-time.sh`);
   - `plumb-shape-cost.vl` fuel;
   - `vl_scaling_shape_test.ts`;
   - seed size.
4. **Shadow before switch.** Where a new producer replaces an old one (L1, L2 stage 3, L6), a
   shadow harness that is off by default runs first and prints every disagreement. A
   disagreement becomes an inventory row, not a merged behaviour change. The disagreement list
   is the step's deliverable.
5. **A step removes or narrows its §7 line in the same PR**, and updates the status table above.
6. **One writer per giant file at a time.** `typecheck.vl`, `emit_classify.vl`, `wasmEmit.vl`
   and `emit_mono.vl` each have one owning lane per wave (see Concurrency).
7. **Fresh builder per PR**, with a handoff note, and up to six concurrent agents (CLAUDE.md).
   Merges go through `cb-audit`.
8. **A PR that advances a lane changes only that lane's state cell** in the status table and **appends one bullet** to the end of its `### <lane> log` under "Lane logs" (never editing an earlier bullet). The merge tooling unions appends, so two PRs on different lanes never conflict.

## Dependency graph

```
L4 assert ──► L4 CheckMark ──┬──► L5 pinDriver ──┐
                             └────────────────────┴──► L9 typecheck carves
S-c (one ast import) ─────────────────────────────────► L9

L8 tyquery.vl ──► L2 stage 2 twins ──┐
                                     ├──► L2 stage 3 rep column ──► RepDesc resume
L1 (SC steps 0→4) ───────────────────┤        ▲
                                     │        └── rulings: literal-union rep, nullable rep
L6 registries by id ─────────────────┘   (removes L1's spelling hop)

L1 SC step 4 ──► L3 family 3 (mono fn-value rules into the checker)
L7 ExpCtx deliveries ──► per-instance compilation (vl test isolation ruling)

T1 host process ABI (done) ──► T3 instrument ports ──► Python retired
L11 budgets inside `vl lint` (needs no spawn) ─► T3 drops the ratchet scripts
owner: optimizer location ──► L10
```

order, subject to the one-writer rule.

## Concurrency

Up to six agents run at once (CLAUDE.md); the owner lifted the CPU cap on 2026-10-12. Rule 6
still holds: one writer per giant file.

- **Lane 1: SC.** B1, then C1(d). It owns `emit_mono.vl` and its parts of `typecheck.vl`
  throughout.
- **The other lanes** each take one step at a time from the earliest wave with an open step,
  choosing a step whose file has no current writer. The intended order per file is:
  - A1 → A2 → C2 → D1 (one continuous claim on `typecheck.vl`, interleaved with SC by area);
  - A3 → B2 → D2 → D3 (`emit_classify.vl`);
  - C3 (`wasmEmit.vl`);
  - A4, A5, A6, A7, B3 and T2, which go to whichever file is free.
- T3 ports need no new std surface, since T1 shipped. A port that does need some goes through std-api review first.

## Owner decisions this plan needs

Each is posted for question time; work that does not depend on it continues.

1. **`one-literal-union-rep`**: RULED 2026-10-13 and applied (#3653): every string literal union reps as the atom.
2. **`nullable-rep-rule-stated-once`**: RULED 2026-10-13, (b): `repOfNullable` becomes a `_`-less `match`.
3. **Where the `-O` optimizer lives (L10).** RULED 2026-10-13, (A): in VL (`optimizer-in-vl.md`), as a pass over the emitted
   module in the compiler. That is what the standing order asks. It also gives the Deno and
   playground hosts `-O`, which they cannot run today, and it removes about 6.5k lines of Rust
   that the host's own manifest says it does not have. If decided this way, skip L10's Rust-only
   first PR (`wasm_bytes`).
4. **September row 19**: a development build resolves the seed from the working directory but
   std from the executable's tree (`SeedOrigin::Cwd`). Recommendation: resolve both from the
   executable's tree.
5. **SC ruling (g)**: the multi-pin case (a binding called at two or more signatures), after the
   table lands. This decides B1's instance record for closures, not whether B1 happens.

- D5531 closes; D5660–D5664 are filed open. This is D3975's shape, a per-call-type instance stored by value, and it is built as a `fn_split` copy per result type, not as a per-call-type return record. A copy allocates nothing, keeps every call direct, and costs a program that is never refused nothing, because the split runs only after a refusal asks for it. A record would add a field read to each call through the result. `monoCoerceRetAtPin` asks `fsAsk` when a refused return has more than one pin past it. `fsPlanFn` then groups the function's calls by result key: the slot keys of the binding a result initializes, or the call's own key. Each key past the first gets a reparsed copy (`reparseFuncDeclAt`, `parser.vl`), and its calls are renamed. The function must be at module scope and called only, never read as a value. One value called at two types, `const a = fw(k)` with `fw` an identity (`fsIdentityArg`), gives the binding a slot per type. The slots past the first hold the identity call's argument, so the call still runs once, in the first slot. The ask comes from the value read's refusal (`wasmEmit.vl`) and from the monomorphizer's un-annotated-argument refusal. Both grading lines run: the alias prints `1 s` and `fw(k)` prints `1 z`. Ten sibling spellings that master refuses run now, among them three types through one maker, a loop, `if`/`else` returning two generics, and `fw` printing first. Seven are still refused. Five are filed: one result called at two types (D5660), a maker with an un-annotated parameter (D5661), a block-local maker (D5662), a maker also read as a value (D5663), and a second identity binding beside the split one (D5664). Of #4000's two sibling rows, the forwarded hole called at two signatures still refuses ("no function type to key a signature from"), and the generic through a generic identity into two bindings already runs on master, as does the relay/`id2`/`inner` spelling. D5472 is a checker literal-row-per-instance error and not this family. The message is spelled when the row is queued; the host ABI (`expInfCount`, `expInfLineAt` and its siblings) reads the queue, so `entry.vl`, the CLI and the oracle are unchanged. Seven `expInf*` columns are deleted from `check_state.vl`. `vl check` over `tests/cases`, base seed against head: 31,433 lines byte-identical (3,954 hints, 5 of them D3817's). `jwSecondPass` is untouched.
- `jwSecondPass` stays out of the driver. Decision rule: join only with difftree identity 0 differ and L2 fuel within +0.5%. What it does that the loop does not: it lexes and parses generated walker source onto the token stream, splices it into the root, rewrites each deep-`is` site (`jwRewriteAll`, whose refusals are positioned type errors), and returns a parse or type rc. Its re-check is a whole fresh `checkProgram`: every phase again and a second `pinReport`. A `checkProgramAgain` keeps the queues and runs no phase. All four callers (`compileSrcOnce`, `checkSrc`, `checkSrcSym`, `modCompile`) run it only when `P.diags` and `T.diags` are empty after `checkProgram` returns, so after `pinReport` has raised the queued refusals. Joining as a fourth `PinPhase` inside the loop would run before `pinReport`, and two witnesses change output. In `gate_both`, D3817's uncalled-export refusal plus a deep-`is` arm that writes its receiver gives one error today, because the refusal is still queued when a loop phase would run. A phase would also raise the rewrite refusal. In D5490's witness, today's pass 2 drops D3817's hint (its `expInfReset`) and reports `const y: i32 = x` as redundant, and following that hint makes the program rc 1. A loop phase would keep the hint, which is a fix, not a refactor. The join that keeps output identical moves the tail into `checkProgram` as a stage after `pinReport` that calls `checkProgram` again. It needs the checker to import the parser and the driver's lexing (`vcLoadToks`, `vcParseBlocked`, `vcDgPush`) and unifies nothing, so it was not built. No candidate was built, so neither difftree nor fuel was run, and fuel does not decide the question. The `expInf*At` readers still index the hint queue directly. A fix for D5490 that keeps D3817's rows across pass 2 must keep D3817 the only solver raising hints, or those readers must filter by solver.
- D5490 closed. `checkProgram` is now `expInfReset` plus `checkProgramResumed`, and `jwSecondPass` calls the second, because pass 1's D3817 annotations are already in the spliced tree, so its hint rows and `expInfPinLets` are still the right answer. The readers' contract is the assertion, not a filter: the hint queue's solver column has its own type, `HintSolver = "expParam"`, so a second solver raising a hint has to widen that type, and the comment there says the readers owe a filter when it does. A filter would turn each `expInf*At(i)` into a scan for the i-th matching row. Fixtures `functions/export-param-{inferred,annotated}-beside-deep-is.vl`; the inferred one fails on master's seed with D5490's stream. L2 fuel 91,411,569,457 (master's seed) to 91,411,569,460. Seed 5,992,553 to 5,992,593 bytes.
- D5593 closed. Pass 2 checks a top-level function's body after every later-declared top-level function it names or dispatches an operator to whose write summary still reads an untyped node (`progCheckCalleeFirst`), each at module scope with the ordinary per-statement sequence; done-on-entry keeps source order around a cycle. The settledness probe exposed one more pre-typing cache: an untyped `Ident` argument in `weArgShapes` (D5591's scalar test) did not count as untyped, so a summary asked early cached `"*"`. `difftree.sh --identity` against #3997's head (`33ba0aca9`): 1 of 15,619 files differs (10,600 built), the new run fixture, which that seed refuses; the new writer-declared-after reject fixture and every inference fixture are identical. D5650 filed: demand inference of a forward callee inside a caller resolves the callee's free names in the caller's scope.
- D5650 closed at the one entry every on-demand path shares, `checkFuncDeclNode`. A top-level declaration's body is checked under `scopeHideEnter`/`scopeHideLeave` (`check_scopes.vl`), which take every frame above the module frame out of `scopeDepthsOf` and put them back in depth order. The caller's locals, parameters, nested functions and narrowing layers are therefore invisible to a forward callee. Thirteen sibling spellings went from silent, invalid wasm or a wrong refusal to the right output, and a caller's `is` narrowing no longer leaks. A/B of the inference, functions, closures, scope, globals, variables and soundness case slices (1,487 cases) against #4001's seed: only the two new fixtures differ.
- D6130 filed open (`profiling-the-compiler.md` §"Measured 2026-10-17"). #4001's callee-first probe took the L2 self-compile from 92.25G to 115.17G (+24.84%). Each probe re-walks the largest call cycle, because an unsettled cycle caches nothing and each body check drops the unsettled summaries. On master `090237334` (128.48G): without the probe and the reorder 101.18G; every later callee checked first, no probe, 96.48G. Three local cuts did not move it (127.76G, 128.60G, 128.66G), so no compiler change shipped.
- D6130 closed in `check_write_effects.vl`. The callee-first probe re-walked the largest call cycle at every probe: an unsettled cycle caches nothing, and every body check drops the unsettled summaries. Each unsettled summary now keeps a witness across `weUnresDrop`, the largest node it read untyped. `weSummaryUnsettled` answers true without a walk while that witness is still untyped, and otherwise walks in probe mode, which ends at the first untyped read and stores no unsettled summary. A build that ran master's full walk beside the new answer disagreed 0 times over the compiler, `scripts`, `std` and 5,538 cases, and its control fired 681 times. A shape summary is a `WeSet` of interned part ids, each part's `@` and `*` flags read once when it is interned, built in a `WeAcc` whose bitset does the dedupe. `weDedupe` and `weClosed` are deleted, `weSubstArgs` and the cycle and argument walks append ids instead of splitting and joining text, and the two narrowing call sites take `callWriteShapes` and `declWriteShapes`, which render the old spelling. Profile of `vl check compiler/entry.vl` on the same source: `fnWriteShapes` inclusive 17.2% of 8,712 samples on master's seed, the family about 6% of 7,985 on the candidate, with `weSplit`, `weDedupe` and `weSubstArgs` gone from the top. L2 fuel 113,619,478,046 (master `6aab28301`'s seed) to 101,976,055,879 (-10.2%), guest allocation 1.88 GB to 0.83 GB, re-baselined. `difftree.sh --identity`: 0 of 15,712 files differ (10,681 built), fixpoint identical. Seed 6,346,336 to 6,373,438 bytes. `vl check compiler/entry.vl` costs about twice the CPU on the candidate (29 s to 61 s) with no more guest samples: the copying heap collects 240 times against 120, each near the end with the live set within 11 KiB of its 512 MiB semispace, because wasmtime grows the heap only when a collection cannot free one request. Under `VL_PUMP_GC=null` both read 32 s, and from a 2 GiB first heap the candidate is the faster (37 s against 41 s). That is the host's growth policy, not filed.
- The host's seed GC heap now grows on headroom (`SeedGcHeadroom`, `scripts/vl-host/src/main.rs`; `gc-heap-policy-2026-09.md` §4). wasmtime 49 grows a copying heap only when one request cannot fit. #4066's seed left about 11 KiB of a 512 MiB semispace free, so it collected every few KiB, and `vl check compiler/entry.vl` doubled its CPU. After any collection leaving more than 75% of a semispace live, the host doubles the heap. It finds out through an epoch tick and the trace log's live size, and grows it with a garbage array wasmtime must grow for. Outputs are byte-identical. Results by workload, master host against this host:
  - `vl check compiler/entry.vl`: master seed 44.7 to 10.5 s CPU, #4066 seed 121.6 to 10.2 s, both at 2.16 GB.
  - L2 build: master seed 65.9 to 25.4 s (264 to 27 collections), #4066 seed 34.8 to 22.6 s (120 to 22). Peak RSS on the #4066 seed rises from 1.12 to 2.17 GB.
  - `vl check compiler/`: medians 234.7 and 240.2 s, at the 4 GiB cap either way.
  
  The epoch check's own cost reads between 0 and about 12% with collection held equal, which this box cannot resolve.
- No write summary is a string any more. The parameter channel (`fnWriteEffects`, `weSums`) and the free-variable channel (`fnWriteFreePaths`) build `WeSet`s through the same `WeAcc`, and `callWriteShapes` and `declWriteShapes` return the set rather than rendering it, so `pathHitsAny` and `shapesHitKey` iterate part ids. A part records the parameter it calls (`wePartParam`) when it is interned, in place of the `@`/`*` text flags and the `star` accumulator. Two string-encoding defects closed: a comma in a string key split a part, so a narrowing survived a write through a parameter, a free variable or an alias and the program trapped (D6400, clause 1), and an `@` in a key read as a parameter call and dropped an unrelated narrowing (D6401, clause 2). `tests/vl_seed_gc_headroom_test.ts` counts the seed's collections over `vl check compiler/check_narrow_flow.vl` under `VL_GC_STATS=1`: 7 under the default policy against 40 under `VL_COMPILE_GC_GROW=off`, bound 20, and the off run is its red control. `difftree.sh --identity`: 0 of 15,748 files differ (10,713 built), fixpoint identical, before the two new fixtures. L2 fuel 101,824,129,379 (master `4cfd7b6b6`'s seed) to 101,638,157,475 (-0.18%). Seed 6,416,301 to 6,410,642 bytes; plumb-shape fuel +1,763 and +6.
- The capture-walk name sets are lists. The after-call, before-loop, condition-drop, right-operand, capture-widen and capture-loss banks are `string[]` per node, extended by copy (`strListWith`) so a clone's bank and a quiet pass's `prior` stay snapshots; the handle memo, the body read lists and the emitter's condition-drop window are lists too, loop paths are `i32[]`, and the deferred covariance slots keep their `string[]`. The defect hunt found three, all through a comma in a map key: D6440 (the after bank split `m["a,b"]`, a trap, on master before #4084 too), D6441 (its split invented the name `x` from `m[",x,"]`, invalid wasm) and D6442 (a substring test dropped `x` from a condition, a loud reject); the last two were exposed by #4084's summaries seeing the comma key's write. difftree `--identity` on master `066d19373`: fixpoint identical, 15,759 files, 3 differ, the three new pins. The checker's 17 `declare` rows and the builtin list's 17 `blPush` rows are each one loop over it, so `tests/vl_host_floor_test.ts` holds three sources rather than five: the table's names to `HOST_FLOOR` and the Deno stubs, its signatures to `HOST_FLOOR`, and it refuses a floor name spelled by hand in `check_core_state.vl` or `driver.vl`. Rust is unchanged apart from a doc comment. Fixpoint holds (5,855,200 bytes, +537). Output identity holds over 15,267 files (10,312 built, 0 differ, 0 uncompared). The distilled corpus lost no runs; its three `trap_loads → trap_loads` movements are the ones master shows. Next: `HOST_FLOOR`'s signature column read from `FS_FLOOR` at host build time, which retires the last cross-language copy.
- `HOST_FLOOR` rows are now (name, handler). `scripts/vl-host/build.rs` parses the `FS_FLOOR` rows of `compiler/check_intrinsics.vl` into `FLOOR_SIGS` in `OUT_DIR` (the build fails on an unparsable row or a missing file), `register_fs_imports` reads the signature from it, and the host build id and CI's host-binary cache key now include that file. Rust tests `floor_tests` refuse a name only one table holds and carry a control that must report three drifts; `tests/vl_host_floor_test.ts` refuses a signature literal in `HOST_FLOOR`. All 17 (name, signature) pairs equal the pre-change hand-written column, and the 36 host-backed fs/args/process/errno/run tests pass on the rebuilt binary. Next: the Deno stubs.
- The Deno harness's 16 hand-listed stub names are gone: `tests/support/hostFloor.ts` parses the `FS_FLOOR` rows of `compiler/check_intrinsics.vl` at load (`parseFloorRows`, which throws on a malformed row or a repeated name) and `runWasm.ts` stubs `FLOOR_NAMES`, with `__proc_exit__` still defined after the spread. Read at load rather than generated, because `runWasm.ts` runs only under Deno with read access and the browser host provides no floor. JS stubs need no signature, so names are all that derive. `tests/vl_host_floor_test.ts` uses the same parser, refuses a floor name spelled as a string in `runWasm.ts` and a harness handler for a name the table lacks, and now carries seven failing controls, one per check, on perturbed copies of the four sources. No `compiler/*.vl` change, so the seed is untouched.

They are `opSelfFnKeyFor` and `opIdxFnFor` calling `checkNode`, and `holeAssignOk` and `fillCoalHoleNodes` calling `assignableExpr`. The cut is a function-valued hook, and it waits for the emitter to give an env parameter only to address-taken functions: today one function value in a `let` moves the whole program to the closure-env convention (seed +3.08%, L2 fuel +7.1%). An eager pass was measured and refuted earlier. Until then the carve goes around the core, and the four force functions stay in `typecheck.vl`.

- Enabler for the hook: the closure-env parameter is per function. Before this, one function value anywhere in a program moved every function onto the env convention.
  - A function now carries the env only when it is lifted or captures (`fnHasEnvParam`, `emit_classify.vl`). Direct calls to every other function use the plain signature and push no null env.
  - An address-taken function without an env is reached through a thunk `(structref env, params…) → return_call f(params…)`. The thunk is declared with the function's `$fnsig` and sits in table slot `gImports + f`, so closure ids, `call_indirect` and funcref equality are unchanged. `return_call` keeps recursion through a value at constant stack.
  - Why a thunk and not the env on every address-taken function: the address-taken set is known only after the bodies are emitted, but a direct call's signature is fixed when its caller is emitted. A thunk takes nothing away from direct calls and cannot under-approximate the set.
  - Exports no longer need the #1265 ABI wrapper: an export names its function directly, and an exported function that has an env is an `emitFail` floor. `blockHasCallRef` no longer reserves the call-ref scratch local for a direct call, which had put an unused local in about 8,659 functions of a compiler with one function value.
  - D3220 moved. Its hole callee called with an extra argument was invalid wasm only because of the env push. Without the push it printed a wrong value, so the direct call now refuses an operand surplus (`directArgsOverflow`), and the row is a loud emit reject.
  - Hook experiment: a temporary `const abiHook: (i32) => i32 = abiId` in `driver.vl`. With master's convention the seed grew 3.1% (5,854,565 to 6,035,028 bytes); with this one it grows 0.21% (to 5,867,104). The built compiler's fuel compiling the hooked source fell from 100,841,253,124 to 99,444,291,183, the same as the unhooked compiler compiling that source (99,444,291,164), so the convention now costs nothing.
  - What is left for the hook: about 12.5 KB of `$fnsig` types, because one is interned for every value-eligible signature (the rec group grows from 158 to 1,170 types), and the types are emitted before the bodies decide the address-taken set. Separately, the compiler does about 5.4% more compile work on any source that holds a function value. That cost is analysis, not convention, and master's seed pays it too.
  - The seed grows 2,744 bytes (5,855,200 to 5,857,944, +1.1% on the size baseline, no rewrite). L2 self-compile fuel moves from 96,497,775,008 to 96,559,073,490 (+0.06%). Two refreshes give the same seed. The distilled corpus lost no runs and moved nothing to silent; its five `rv3310_*` `trap_loads → trap_loads` message movements show on master's seed as well. The filed witnesses read 2843 of 2843 as filed. A new fixture, `tests/cases/closures/env-param-only-where-read.vl`, runs the same on master and covers a struct field, a generic, a returned value, recursion through a value, an export, a block-local function and a capture.
- The hook was built and not landed; D5017 blocks it. `check_core_state.vl` held two fn-typed slots, `checkDeclFn` and `deliverExprFn`, installed by `checkProgramPass`; the four force edges called through them, and with those edges cut the core is 88 declarations and 10,475 lines.
  - With the hook, `vl check compiler/entry.vl` refuses two reads of `e.asMode` in `emitAsCast` (`wasmEmit.vl`): "the call to `asExprIsUnionCastIn` can write it". The deliver slot alone is enough. The path runs `exprUnion` → `substHoleTyReal` → `opSelfFnKeyFor` → `checkNode` → `holeAssignOk`, and the slot call is a call through a function value.
  - `callDeclFnIx` resolves a function value only when it is a binding bound once to a lambda, so a slot holding a named function makes the summary "may write anything". The same refusal reaches user programs on master, even through a `const` bound to a named function (D5017's witness and ablation).
  - Hoisting `asMode` into locals in `emitAsCast` would build, but it hides the false kill rather than fixing it. The fix is a behaviour change, and so it needs its own PR: either the store set of a module fn binding (its initialiser plus every value a setter can store), or the union of the write shapes of every address-taken function.
- D5017 closed. A call through a binding is charged the union of every function stored into it: the initialiser, each `name = v`, and for a parameter the argument each direct call hands it (`cwFnValueTargets`, `check_write_scan.vl`). A set that cannot be known (a value from a list, a field or a call result; a setter reached as a value) still writes anything, which is D5018. The frame walk also stopped walking a merged root's statements twice, which had made every module-scope name in a multi-module program look declared twice. With the hook diff applied, `vl check compiler/entry.vl` checks clean.
- The hook was priced against master (11df0cd86) and not landed: its cost is over the 0.5% bar. Both spellings build in about 40 s at each bootstrap level and reach a fixpoint, so the old `ffI32Merge` non-termination did not reproduce.
  - The setter spelling, `checkerHooksSet(check, deliver)` with fn-typed parameters, grows the seed 12,803 bytes (+0.22%) and L2 self-compile fuel 9.31% (99,883,349,859 to 109,180,531,483). It turns on monomorphisation and `fn_flow` over the compiler: `monomorphize` goes from 28 to 1,646 inclusive profile samples, and `ffBuild`/`ffWalkFn` from 0 to about 500.
  - The setter-less spelling has two `export let` slots in `check_core_state.vl`, assigned in `checkProgramPass`. It grows the seed 12,762 bytes (+0.22%), most of it the type section (+12,442), and fuel 4.37% (to 104,251,503,021).
  - That cost is the module-wide `fnValUsed` flag turning true, which enables about 67 gated emit paths. Capture analysis is about 3.1 points of it (`capComputeNamesOf`, `capScan`, `capNarrowRecordFrame`, `capCacheBuild`, `capturedKindOf`, `collectCloSigs`), measured by an unsound early return for a function with no parent frame. The other gates (`cloLiftDecide`, `blockHasCallRef` and others) are about 1.3 points.
  - The hooked seed compiling master's source spends exactly master's fuel, so the cost comes from compiling a function value, not from slower generated code. `plumb-shape-cost.vl --check` moves +22 fuel units over master's own reading.
  - What would make the hook cheap is gating closure analysis on a nested or capturing frame existing, rather than on any function value existing. The `$fnsig` type growth comes from the same flag.
  - `tests/cases/operators/overload-declared-after-use.vl` is the first fixture that reaches the two operator-force sites, `opIdxFnFor` for `[]` and `opSelfFnKeyFor` for `+`. This was checked with a compiler that traps at both sites; none of the 773 existing files that declare an operator reached either one. The hole-refill edges (`holeAssignOk`, `fillCoalHoleNodes`) are already reached by many cases.
- The closure gates are split. `fnValUsed` still means "a function value exists", and the new `fnNestUsed` means "a lambda or nested function was lifted". Capture analysis, the closure lift and unbox, captured reads and env slots, and `capCacheBuild` are gated on `fnNestUsed`.
  - Without a nested frame, `collectCloSigs` interns a function's own `$fnsig` only for a declared function named outside a call's callee, or for a function the emitter added.
  - With no nested frame, a concrete higher-order function with an un-annotated return no longer opens the mono pass, because `monoSpecializeConcrete` declines it before reading an argument. That was most of the hook's cost: the setter has fn-typed parameters and no return annotation. With a lambda present the pass still runs, because it also prunes uncalled lambdas.
  - Output identity, against the fixpoint of master: 224 modules differ, and every one runs with the same exit code and output. 219 lose 1 to 42 `$fnsig` types, 4 keep the same types in a different order, and in `modules/std-testing-fail` the trap backtrace's byte offsets move. No module grew.
  - D4891 moves from clause 1 to clause 2. Its call `fs[0](1)` is keyed as a nullary void call; master found that key only because `main`'s own sig was interned, and called through the wrong sig. The candidate does not intern it, so the same mis-keyed call is now a loud emit reject. The row stays open.
  - L2 self-compile fuel against master at 2026f3704:

    | build | fuel | seed bytes |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | master | 98,606,856,745 | 5,919,107 |
    | master + hook | 108,985,494,771 (+10.53%) | 5,932,022 |
    | candidate | 98,487,369,569 (−0.12%) | 5,922,614 |
    | candidate + hook | 99,138,499,698 (+0.54%) | 5,922,995 |

  - The reading moves with the compiled source alone: one compiler compiling a 40-line rewrite of `collectCloSigs` read +0.52% more fuel than on the version kept. At this resolution, a difference of half a percent is noise from the source's shape.
  - `plumb-shape-cost.vl --check` fuel is lower than master: main 8,550,779,219 against 8,653,307,223 (−1.2%), and tail 6,176,765,037 against 6,249,247,227 (−1.2%). There was no re-baseline.
  - If the hook's setter gets a return annotation (`: void`), it reopens the mono pass.
- The hook landed as ONE slot, `checkerForceFn` in `check_core_state.vl`, which `checkProgramPass` installs; the force sites (`opSelfFnKeyFor`, `opIdxFnFor`, `holeAssignOk`, `fillCoalHoleNodes`) call through it, so the relation core no longer imports the core checker. Seven modules then took 17,550 lines (486 declarations) out of `typecheck.vl` (29,495 → 11,945): `check_relate_core.vl` (relation, joins, substitution, `tErr*`; 4,849), `check_annot_resolve.vl` (757), `check_fit_rules.vl` (3,667), `check_ret_name_shapes.vl` (617), `check_narrow_flow.vl` (4,130), `check_node_rules.vl` (1,544) and `check_demands.vl` (3,483).
  - The seed is 5,922,931 bytes, +317 over master. `difftree.sh --identity` found the candidate compiling master's source to master's fixpoint, and 0 of 15,273 files differ (10,317 built).
  - `typecheck.vl` re-exports `assignable`, `bindGenWalk`, `ovPickFor`, `substTyDeep` and `tyProvesNonNull` for `emit_mono.vl` (an L6 file); point that import at the new homes once L6 lands.
  - L2 self-compile fuel is +2.33% over master, past the 0.6% budget. The compilers are equally fast: one source compiled by master's seed and by the candidate reads the same within 0.0003%. The cost is the source's shape: the hook alone is +0.51%, and the carve adds +1.80%.
  - The carve's cost grows with the lines moved, about 0.1% per 1,000. Measured parts: the new import names (about 900, +0.26%), the new exports (243, +0.10%) and declaration order (+0.16%); the rest was not localised. So any carve of 8,000 lines or more misses a 0.6% budget, and the budget for big carves needs deciding.
  - `plumb-shape-cost.vl --check` passes, with fuel −1.2% against its baseline on both units. The per-file ladder and sentinel baselines were re-split with their totals unchanged (ladder 341 / 6, sentinel 474).
- The five re-exports `typecheck.vl` kept for `emit_mono.vl` are gone. `emit_mono.vl` imports `assignable`, `bindGenWalk`, `substTyDeep` and `tyProvesNonNull` from `check_relate_core.vl` and `ovPickFor` from `check_demands.vl`; `typecheck.vl` imports the ones it uses itself. No other file reached them through `typecheck.vl`. The fixpoint seed is byte-identical to master's (5,924,421 bytes, delta 0), and `difftree.sh --identity` reads 0 of 15,273 files differ (10,317 built).
- The fuel budget for big carves is decided: a carve may add up to +1.5% L2 self-compile fuel when the cost comes from source shape alone (more modules, more imports) and `difftree.sh --identity` reads 0 differ. Past that, the cost is found (a hot cross-module call that lost inlining, per-import-name cost in `driver.vl`) before the carve ships. The per-kind checkers then left `typecheck.vl` (11,943 → 429 lines), which keeps only `checkNode` and its dispatcher `checkNodeReal`.
  - All 88 functions left were one strongly connected component, and every cycle passed through `checkNode`, so no callee-closed carve existed. The moved code calls `checkNodeHook(ix)` (`check_core_state.vl`), which goes through the `checkerForceFn` slot `checkProgramPass` already installs (kind 0). That one-line forwarder is the only new mechanism; the rest is moves, `export`s and imports.
  - The new modules, with their line counts after fmt: `check_assignable.vl` (1,937), `check_operators.vl` (1,686), `check_calls.vl` (1,535), `check_statements.vl` (1,303), `check_member_call.vl` (1,276), `check_fn_decl.vl` (1,183), `check_inferred_return.vl` (1,099), `check_match.vl` (818), `check_top_level.vl` (809), `check_access.vl` (644) and `check_container_lits.vl` (165). `check_program.vl` and `check_emit_queries.vl` import `assignableExpr`, `fsbRecheckFrom` and `monoRecheckBegin` from `check_assignable.vl`.
  - L2 self-compile fuel goes from 90,565,923,060 on master to 89,333,316,998 (−1.36%). The seed is 5,943,729 bytes, +70 over master, and it is a fixpoint (L1 == L2). `difftree.sh --identity` reads 0 of 15,276 files differ (10,320 built). `plumb-shape-cost.vl --check` passes (tail fuel −1.2%). The ladder and sentinel baselines were re-split per file with their totals unchanged (341 / 6 and 469), and a second `--write-baseline` leaves both files unchanged.
- The hook census, after the per-kind carve: 111 call sites in 12 modules reach the checker core through `checkerForceFn`, and none can be a direct import.
  - Kind 0 (`checkNode`) has 109 sites: 100 check a child node of the node being checked, 6 force a later declaration's pending return (`opSelfFnKeyFor`, `opIdxFnFor`, two in `check_calls.vl`, one each in `check_operators.vl` and `check_member_call.vl`), and 3 re-check a node rewritten in place (`check_access.vl` twice, `check_statements.vl` once). Kind 1 (`assignableExpr`) has 2: `holeAssignOk` and `fillCoalHoleNodes`.
  - Every kind-0 caller is among the 137 modules `typecheck.vl` reaches, and both kind-1 callers (`check_demands.vl`, `check_relate_core.vl`) are among the modules `check_assignable.vl` reaches. The calls are the recursion itself, so no helper move breaks a cycle: a direct edge needs the dispatcher and the per-kind checker in one module, which undoes the carve. A declaration force cannot call `checkFuncDeclNode` directly either, since it would skip `checkNode`'s tail-position, observer and recording steps.
  - Nothing in the code moved. Moving `checkerForce` and its install from `check_program.vl` into `typecheck.vl` beside `checkNode`, with no other change, cost +0.44% L2 fuel (89,918,239,511 against master's 89,520,888,450) and bought only a tidier owner for the slot, so it was dropped. Routing the two hot kind-0 reads in `check_demands.vl` and `check_relate_core.vl` through `checkNodeHook` was dropped as well. Those four reads stay direct `checkerForceFn` calls, and `checkProgramPass` still installs the slot.
  - `typecheck.vl` keeps `checkNode`, `checkNodeReal` and the install: it is the one module that imports every per-kind checker, so the dispatcher belongs there. The literal and identifier arms stay inline; the identifier arm re-dispatches an inlined `const` literal, which a lower module could reach only through the slot.
  - The comments on `checkerForceFn`, `checkNodeHook` and `checkNodeReal` now say what the slot carries and why it is a slot, and the seed is byte-identical to master's fixpoint (5,955,790 bytes).

### T6 log

It needs the same tree and a clean checkout, the same seed sha256, deno pinned by `.dvmrc`, the same rustc, and every job passed with every shard present. With `GATE_CI_PROOF=<sha>`, `gate.sh` then skips the 15 rows that CI runs, and the corpus oracle. That is about 1,100 of the gate's 1,750–1,900 CPU-s. With the variable unset, the gate behaves as before. `tests/ci_proof_test.ts` refuses 28 mutated inputs.

### S5 log

- Step 4: `compiler/iface_hash.vl` computes a per-module interface hash and skips nothing yet. It covers the tokens with each fully annotated, non-generic body cut out, the names each cut body mentions and how, every top-level binding's checked type, and a deep hash over imports. Under Q4 (A), generic, untyped-parameter and inferred-return bodies stay in. `tests/vl_iface_hash_test.ts` grades 30 single-edit pairs with 0 unsound and 8 hits, and dropping each of the six ingredients turns its witness unsound. The history hit rate is 2 of 15 compiler edits (13.3%), 4 of 15 with inferred-return bodies cut, both with 0 unsound. Seed 6,168,691 → 6,181,799 bytes. Next: bank the edited module's importer demands (Q1 B adoption, D3817 parameters, instantiations) so that a dependent's re-check can be skipped.
- Step 4b: the interface hash cuts inferred-return bodies and lets their checked type carry them, with the body folded back in when the type renders elided.
